183: Independent Auditor’s Report on Internal Control over Financial
183: Reporting and on Compliance and Other Matters Based on an
183: Audit of Financial Statements Performed in Accordance
183: with Government Auditing Standards
183: To the School Board and Management of
183: Houston Independent School District
183: Houston, Texas
183: We have audited, in accordance with auditing standards generally accepted in the United States of America and the
183: standards applicable to financial audits contained in Government Auditing Standards issued by the Comptroller
183: General of the United States (Government Auditing Standards), the financial statements of the governmental
183: activities, the business-type activities, each major fund, and the aggregate remaining fund information of Houston
183: Independent School District (the District) as of and for the year ended June 30, 2025, and the related notes to the
183: financial statements, which collectively comprise the District’s basic financial statements, and have issued our report
183: thereon dated January 12, 2026.
183: Report on Internal Control over Financial Reporting
183: In planning and performing our audit of the financial statements, we considered the District’s internal control over
183: financial reporting (internal control) as a basis for designing audit procedures that are appropriate in the
183: circumstances for the purpose of expressing our opinions on the financial statements, but not for the purpose of
183: expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of the District’s internal control. Accordingly, we do not express an
183: opinion on the effectiveness of the District’s internal control.
183: A deficiency in internal control exists when the design or operation of a control does not allow management or
183: employees in the normal course of performing their assigned functions, to prevent, or detect and correct
183: misstatements on a timely basis. A material weakness is a deficiency, or a combination of deficiencies, in internal
183: control, such that there is a reasonable possibility that a material misstatement of the entity’s financial statements
183: will not be prevented, or detected and corrected on a timely basis. A significant deficiency is a deficiency, or a
183: combination of deficiencies, in internal control that is less severe than a material weakness, yet important enough
183: to merit attention by those charged with governance.
183: Our consideration of internal control was for the limited purpose described in the first paragraph of this section and
183: was not designed to identify all deficiencies in internal control that might be material weaknesses or significant
183: deficiencies and therefore, material weaknesses or significant deficiencies may exist that have not been identified.
183: Given these limitations, during our audit we did not identify any deficiencies in internal control that we consider to
183: be material weaknesses. We identified a certain deficiency in internal control, described in the accompanying
183: schedule of findings and questioned costs as item 2025-001, that we consider to be a significant deficiency.
183: Report on Compliance and Other Matters
183: As part of obtaining reasonable assurance about whether the District’s financial statements are free from material
183: misstatement, we performed tests of its compliance with certain provisions of laws, regulations, contracts, and grant
183: agreements, noncompliance with which could have a direct and material effect on the financial statements. However,
183: providing an opinion on compliance with those provisions was not an objective of our audit and, accordingly, we
183: do not express such an opinion. The results of our tests disclosed no instances of noncompliance or other matters
183: that are required to be reported under Government Auditing Standards.
183: Independent Auditor’s Report on Compliance for Each Major Federal and State Program and Report on Internal Control over Compliance Required by the Uniform Guidance and Texas Grant
183: Management Standards
184: - 154 -
184: The School Board and Management of
184: Houston Independent School District
184: The District’s Response to Findings
184: Government Auditing Standards requires the auditor to perform limited procedures on the District’s response to
184: the findings identified in our audit and described in the accompanying schedule of findings and questioned costs.
184: The District’s response was not subjected to the other auditing procedures applied in the audit of the financial
184: statements and, accordingly, we express no opinion on it.
184: Purpose of this Report
184: The purpose of this report is solely to describe the scope of our testing of internal control and compliance and the
184: results of that testing, and not to provide an opinion on the effectiveness of the District’s internal control or on
184: compliance. This report is an integral part of an audit performed in accordance with Government Auditing Standards
184: in considering the District’s internal control and compliance. Accordingly, this communication is not suitable for any
184: other purpose.

185: Independent Auditor’s Report on Compliance for Each Major Federal and State
185: Program and Report on Internal Control over Compliance
185: Required by the Uniform Guidance and Texas Grant Management Standards
185: To the School Board and Management of

185: Houston, Texas
185: Report on Compliance for Each Major Federal Program and State Program
185: Opinion on Each Major Federal and State Program
185: We have audited Houston Independent School District’s (the District) compliance with the types of compliance
185: requirements described in the U.S. Office of Management and Budget (OMB) Compliance Supplement and the Texas
185: Grant Management Standards that could have a direct and material effect on each of the District’s major federal
185: and state programs for the year ended June 30, 2025. The District’s major federal and state programs are identified
185: in the summary of auditor’s results section of the accompanying schedule of findings and questioned costs.
185: In our opinion, the District complied, in all material respects, with the compliance requirements referred to above
185: that could have a direct and material effect on each of its major federal and state programs for the year ended
185: June 30, 2025.
185: Basis for Opinion on Each Major Federal and State Program
185: We conducted our audit of compliance in accordance with auditing standards generally accepted in the United
185: States of America (GAAS); the standards applicable to financial audits contained in Government Auditing Standards
185: issued by the Comptroller General of the United States (Government Auditing Standards); the audit requirements
185: of Title 2 U.S. Code of Federal Regulations Part 200, Uniform Administrative Requirements, Cost Principles, and Audit
185: Requirements for Federal Awards (Uniform Guidance) and the Texas Grant Management Standards (TxGMS). Our
185: responsibilities under those standards, the Uniform Guidance and TxGMS are further described in the Auditor’s
185: Responsibilities for the Audit of Compliance section of our report.
185: We are required to be independent of the District and to meet our other ethical responsibilities, in accordance with
185: relevant ethical requirements relating to our audit. We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient
185: and appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion on compliance for each major federal and state program. Our
185: audit does not provide a legal determination of the District’s compliance with the compliance requirements referred
185: to above.
185: Responsibilities of Management for Compliance
185: Management is responsible for compliance with the requirements referred to above and for the design,
185: implementation, and maintenance of effective internal control over compliance with the requirements of laws,
185: statutes, regulations, rules and provisions of contracts or grant agreements applicable to the District’s federal and
185: state programs.
186: - 156 -
186: The School Board and Management of
186: Houston Independent School District
186: Auditor’s Responsibilities for the Audit of Compliance
186: Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether material noncompliance with the compliance
186: requirements referred to above occurred, whether due to fraud or error, and express an opinion on the District’s
186: compliance based on our audit. Reasonable assurance is a high level of assurance but is not absolute assurance and
186: therefore is not a guarantee that an audit conducted in accordance with GAAS, Government Auditing Standards,
186: the Uniform Guidance and TxGMS will always detect material noncompliance when it exists. The risk of not detecting
186: material noncompliance resulting from fraud is higher than for that resulting from error, as fraud may involve
186: collusion, forgery, intentional omissions, misrepresentations, or the override of internal control. Noncompliance with
186: the compliance requirements referred to above is considered material, if there is a substantial likelihood that,
186: individually or in the aggregate, it would influence the judgment made by a reasonable user of the report on
186: compliance about the District’s compliance with the requirements of each major federal and state program as a
186: whole.
186: In performing an audit in accordance with GAAS, Government Auditing Standards, the Uniform Guidance and
186: TxGMS, we:
186:  Exercise professional judgment and maintain professional skepticism throughout the audit.
186:  Identify and assess the risks of material noncompliance, whether due to fraud or error, and design and
186: perform audit procedures responsive to those risks. Such procedures include examining, on a test basis,
186: evidence regarding the District’s compliance with the compliance requirements referred to above and
186: performing such other procedures as we considered necessary in the circumstances.
186:  Obtain an understanding of the District’s internal control over compliance relevant to the audit in order to
186: design audit procedures that are appropriate in the circumstances and to test and report on internal control
186: over compliance in accordance with the Uniform Guidance and TxGMS, but not for the purpose of
186: expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of the District’s internal control over compliance. Accordingly,
186: no such opinion is expressed.
186: We are required to communicate with those charged with governance regarding, among other matters, the planned
186: scope and timing of the audit and any significant deficiencies and material weaknesses in internal control over
186: compliance that we identified during the audit.
186: Other Matters
186: The results of our auditing procedures disclosed instances of noncompliance which are required to be reported in
186: accordance with the Uniform Guidance and which are described in the accompanying schedule of findings and
186: questioned costs as item 2025-002. Our opinion on each major federal program is not modified with respect to
186: these matters. Government Auditing Standards require the auditor to perform limited procedures on the District’s
186: response to the noncompliance findings identified in our compliance audit described in the accompanying schedule
186: of findings and questioned costs. The District’s response was not subjected to the other auditing procedures applied
186: in the audit of compliance and, accordingly, we express no opinion on the response.
186: Report on Internal Control over Compliance
186: Our consideration of internal control over compliance was for the limited purpose described in the Auditor’s
186: Responsibilities for the Audit of Compliance section above and was not designed to identify all deficiencies in
186: internal control over compliance that might be material weaknesses or significant deficiencies in internal control
186: over compliance and therefore, material weaknesses or significant deficiencies may exist that were not identified.
186: We did not identify any deficiencies in internal control over compliance that we consider to be material weaknesses.
186: However, as discussed below, we did identify certain deficiencies in internal control over compliance that we
186: consider to be significant deficiencies.
187: - 157 -
187: The School Board and Management of
187: Houston Independent School District
187: A deficiency in internal control over compliance exists when the design or operation of a control over compliance
187: does not allow management or employees, in the normal course of performing their assigned functions, to prevent,
187: or detect and correct, noncompliance with a type of compliance requirement of a federal or state program on a
187: timely basis. A materialweakness in internalcontrolover compliance is a deficiency, or a combination of deficiencies,
187: in internal control over compliance, such that there is a reasonable possibility that material noncompliance with a
187: type of compliance requirement of a federal or state program will not be prevented, or detected and corrected, on
187: a timely basis. A significant deficiency in internal control over compliance is a deficiency, or a combination of
187: deficiencies, in internal control over compliance with a type of compliance requirement of a federal or state program
187: that is less severe than a material weakness in internal control over compliance, yet important enough to merit
187: attention by those charged with governance. We consider the deficiencies in internal control over compliance
187: described in the accompanying schedule of findings and questioned costs as items 2025-002 and 2025-003, to be
187: significant deficiencies.
187: Our audit was not designed for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of internal control over
187: compliance. Accordingly, no such opinion is expressed. Government Auditing Standards requires the auditor to
187: perform limited procedures on the District’s response to the internal control over compliance findings identified in
187: our compliance audit described in the accompanying schedule of findings and questioned costs. The District’s
187: response was not subjected to the other auditing procedures applied in the audit of compliance and, accordingly,
187: we express no opinion on the response.
187: The purpose of this report on internal control over compliance is solely to describe the scope of our testing of
187: internal control over compliance and the results of that testing based on the requirements of the Uniform Guidance
187: and TxGMS. Accordingly, this report is not suitable for any other purpose.

190: H O U S T O N I N D E P E N D E N T
190: S C H O O L D I S T R I C T
190: Houston Independent School District
190: Schedule of Findings and Questioned Costs
190: For the Fiscal Year Ended June 30, 2025
190: Section II – Findings Related to Financial Statements
190: 2025-001
190: Significant deficiency in Internal Control over Financial Reporting: School Health And Related Services
190: (SHARS) Revenue
190: Criteria
190: Management is responsible for the accuracy and completeness of all financial records and related information and
190: for establishing and maintaining effective internal control over financial reporting. Reimbursement requests for
190: SHARS revenue must meet the requirements applicable under federal and state laws. The Texas Health and Human
190: Services Commission (HHSC) reviews the reimbursement requests and provides estimated Cost Settlement reports
190: identifying any unallowable amounts. Any amounts determined to be unallowable should not be recognized as
190: revenue in the reporting entity’s financial statements.
190: Condition
190: The estimated SHARS claims revenue and accrual recognized in the District's financial statements for the year ended
190: June 30, 2025 were misstated due to failure to consider the HHSC 2023 Cost Settlement report findings and results
190: in evaluation of the estimates.
190: Cause
190: The HHSC 2023 Cost Settlement Report was received by the District during the year ended June 30, 2025 and was
190: not properly evaluated to determine if an adjustment should be recorded in the financial statements. The 2023 Cost
190: Settlement Report was not provided timely to the Controller’s Office for review.
190: Effect
190: Misstatement of the District’s financial statements was not prevented, or detected and corrected, by the District’s
190: system of internal control. Failure to establish effective monitoring and closing procedures will allow possible
190: irregularities to exist and continue without notice.
190: Recommendation
190: We recommend that the District implement additional procedures to ensure all Cost Settlement reports are provided
190: to the Controller’s Office to ensure proper evaluation of the estimated revenue adjustments in accordance with
190: generally accepted accounting principles.
190: Views of Responsible Officials and Planned Corrective Actions
190: See corrective action plan

191: - 161 -
191: H O U S T O N I N D E P E N D E N T
191: S C H O O L D I S T R I C T
191: Houston Independent School District
191: Schedule of Findings and Questioned Costs
191: For the Fiscal Year Ended June 30, 2025
191: Section III – Federal and State Award Findings and Questioned Costs
191: 2025-002
191: Information on Federal Program:
191: Federal Program: COVID-19 – Elementary and Secondary School Emergency Relief Fund (ARP ESSER III)
191: Federal Agency: U.S. Department of Education
191: Federal Award Identification Number and Year: 21528001101912 & 21533002101912 - 2021
191: Passthrough Agency: Texas Education Agency
191: Assistance Listing: 84.425U & 84.425W
191: Compliance Requirement: Equipment
191: Significant deficiency in Internal Control over Compliance and Noncompliance
191: Criteria
191: In accordance with federal regulations, under 2 CFR § 200.313, a non-Federal entity must conduct a physical
191: inventory of all equipment purchased with federal funds at least once every two years. Furthermore, under 2 CFR §
191: 200.303, the entity must maintain effective internal controls designed to ensure compliance with federal
191: requirements for safeguarding assets.
191: Condition
191: A physical inventory count was not performed for all school campuses within the District during the last two years,
191: with equipment purchased with federal grant awards.
191: Cause
191: A recent inventory of capital assets acquired with federal awards, performed by the District as of June 30, 2024, had
191: incomplete participation from approximately 45% of the District's campuses. The District’s internal controls over
191: compliance failed to trigger action to verify sufficient participation and completeness, to prevent or detect and
191: correct noncompliance.
191: Effect
191: The District was not in compliance with the physical inventory requirements of the Uniform Guidance. Possible
191: effects include unaccounted for equipment purchased with federal funds.
191: Questioned Cost
191: None
191: Context
191: During our audit, 20 out of the 25 assets we selected to verify if a physical inventory count took place were either
191: shown as “unaccounted for” or excluded from the counts performed. The heating, ventilation and cooling
191: equipment purchased and added to school campuses was excluded from the counts performed and totaled 6 units.
191: The remaining items were primarily buses and band instruments which were inventoried at schools that did not
191: count or were noted as “unaccounted for” during the inventory count process.
191: Recommendation
191: We recommend the District provide training for campus fixed asset administrators on inventory procedures
191: including inventory audit and reconciliation practices to ensure timely completion of inventory count procedures at
191: all campuses.
191: Views of Responsible Officials and Planned Corrective Actions
191: See corrective action plan
191: Section III – Federal and State Award Findings and Questioned Costs
192: - 162 -
192: H O U S T O N I N D E P E N D E N T
192: S C H O O L D I S T R I C T
192: Houston Independent School District
192: Schedule of Findings and Questioned Costs
192: For the Fiscal Year Ended June 30, 2025
192: 2025-003
192: Information on Federal Program:
192: Federal Program: COVID-19 – Elementary and Secondary School Emergency Relief Fund (ARP ESSER III)
192: Federal Agency: U.S. Department of Education
192: Federal Award Identification Number and Year: 215280587110070 - 2021
192: Passthrough Agency: Texas Education Agency
192: Assistance Listing: 84.425U & 84.425W
192: Compliance Requirement: Allowable Cost/Cost Principals
192: Significant deficiency in Internal Control over Compliance and Questioned Costs
192: Criteria
192: Grant award terms provide a start and end date of the federal award and all expenditures must be incurred during
192: the grant period, including any liquidation period.
192: Condition
192: For the TCLAS ARP ESSER III grant, expenditures were incurred after the liquidation period ended on November 30,
192: 2024.The approval of subscription costs was not obtained for liquidation extension as required by the Department
192: of Education.
192: Cause
192: The District was required to liquidate approximately $450 thousand of remaining TCLAS High-Quality After-School
192: funds within five months of its fiscal year. During this period, the existing expenditure review process did not provide
192: sufficient oversight to prevent costs related to the period beyond the liquidation deadline from being processed.
192: Effect
192: The District’s internal controls failed to ensure expenses were compliant with grant-specific restrictions before
192: payment and therefore the District incurred and charged unallowable costs to the federal program.
192: Questioned Cost
192: $54,626 of subscription services expenditures
192: Context
192: During our audit, 3 of 60 expenditures we selected for testing were associated with software subscription contracts
192: that had service periods which commenced prior to the liquidation period deadline but extended beyond the
192: standard grant liquidation period deadline of November 30, 2024.
192: Recommendation
192: We recommend the District to provide training for staff responsible for reviewing grant expenditures on federal cost
192: principles and the specific restrictions of grant awards.
192: Views of Responsible Officials and Planned Corrective Actions
192: See corrective action plan

199: H O U S T O N I N D E P E N D E N T
199: S C H O O L D I S T R I C T
199: Notes to Schedule of Expenditures of Federal and State Awards
199: HOUSTON INDEPENDENT SCHOOL DISTRICT
199: NOTES TO SCHEDULE OF EXPENDITURES OF FEDERAL AND STATE AWARDS
199: JUNE 30, 2025
199: 1. The Houston Independent School District (the “District”) utilizes the funds specified in the Texas Education
199: Agency Financial Accountability System Resource Guide. The information in the Schedule of Expenditures
199: of Federal Awards (SEFA) is presented in accordance with the requirements of the Uniform Guidance and
199: the Schedule of Expenditures of State Awards (SESA) is presented in accordance with the requirements of
199: Texas Uniform Grant Management Standards.
199: The Special Revenue Fund is used to account for resources restricted to, or designated for, specific purposes
199: by a grantor. Federal and state financial assistance generally is accounted for in a Special Revenue Fund.
199: Generally, unused balances are returned to the grantor at the close of specified project periods.
199: 2. The accounting and financial reporting treatment applied to a fund is determined by its measurement focus.
199: The governmental funds are accounted for using a current-financial-resources measurement focus. All federal
199: and state grant funds are accounted for in the Special Revenue Fund, a component of the governmental
199: funds. With this measurement focus, only current assets and current liabilities, generally, are included on the
199: balance sheet. Operating statements of these funds present increases (i.e., revenues and other financing
199: sources) and decreases (i.e., expenditures and other financing uses) in net current assets.
199: The modified-accrual basis of accounting is used for the governmental funds. This basis of accounting
199: recognizes revenues in the accounting period in which they become both measurable and available and
199: expenditures in the accounting period in which the fund liability is incurred, except that principal and interest
199: on general long-term debt are recognized when due.
199: National School Lunch Program and Summer Food Service Program non-cash commodities are recorded at
199: their estimated market value at the time of donation.
199: Federal and state grant funds are considered to be earned when all eligibility requirements have been met.
199: Any excess of revenues or expenditures is recorded as unearned revenues or accounts receivable,
199: respectively.
199: 3. The period of availability for federal funds for the purpose of liquidating all financial obligations incurred on or
199: before the ending date of the federal project period extended 120 calendar days beyond the federal project
199: period ending date as specified in the terms and conditions (2 CFR section 200.344(b)).
199: 4. The disbursement of funds received under federal grant programs generally requires compliance with terms
199: and conditions specified in the grant agreements and is subject to audit by the grantor agencies. Any
199: disallowed claims resulting from such audits could become a liability of the General Fund. In the opinion of
199: management, such disallowed claims, if any, will not have a material effect on any of the financial statements
199: of the individual fund types or on the overall financial position of the District at June 30, 2025.
199: Total Expenditures of Federal Financial Assistance: $ 389,630,012
199: Clean School Bus Rebates 5,625,000
199: Prior Year Unavailable Revenue FEMA-DR-4485 Disaster Grant-Public Assistance 3,001,482
199: Deferred Revenue FEMA-DR-4781 Disaster Grant-Public Assistance (845,807)
199: Additional federal revenue reported
199: Interest Subsidy on Build America & Qualified School Construction Bonds 3,941,023
199: School Health and Related Services (SHARS) 194,085
199: Total Federal Revenues Reported $ 401,545,795

199: Federal Awards (SEFA) is presented in accordance with the requirements of the Uniform Guidance and the
199: Schedule of Expenditures of StateAwards (SESA) is presented in accordance with the requirements of Texas
199: Uniform Grant Management Standards.

199: by a grantor. Federal and state financial assistance generally is accounted for in a Special Revenue Fund.
199: Generally, unused balances are returned to the grantor at the close of specified project periods.
199: 2.The accounting and financial reporting treatment applied to a fund is determined by its measurement focus.
199: The governmental funds are accounted for using a current-financial-resources measurement focus.All federal
199: and state grant funds are accounted for in the Special Revenue Fund, a component of the governmental
199: funds. With this measurement focus, only current assets and current liabilities, generally, are included on the
199: balance sheet. Operating statements of these funds present increases (i.e., revenues and other financing
199: sources) and decreases (i.e., expenditures and other financing uses) in net current assets.
199: The modified-accrual basis of accounting is used for the governmental funds. This basis of accounting
199: recognizes revenues in the accounting period in which they become both measurable and available and

199: Federalandstategrantfundsareconsideredtobeearnedwhenalleligibilityrequirementshavebeenmet.Any
199: excess of revenues or expenditures is recorded as unearned revenues or accounts receivable, respectively.
199: 3.The period of availability for federal funds for the purpose of liquidating all financial obligations incurred on or
199: before the ending date of the federal project period extended 120 calendar days beyond the federal project
199: period ending date as specified in the terms and conditions (2 CFR section 200.344(b)).
199: 4.The disbursement of funds received under federal grant programs generally requires compliance with
199: terms and conditions specified in the grant agreements and is subject to audit by the grantor agencies. Any
199: disallowed claims resulting from such audits could become a liability of the General Fund. In the opinion of
199: management, such disallowed claims, if any, will not have a material effect on any of the financial statements
199: of the individual fund types or on the overall financial position of the District at June 30, 2025.
199: 5.The District has elected not to use the 10-percent de minimis indirect cost as allowed under the Uniform
199: Guidance.
199: 6.Indirect costs in the amount of $357,648 from the prior fiscal year are included in the Schedule of Expenditures
199: of Federal Awards.
